- 2026-09-10 — Three places where this batch implements LESS than the round-1 report proposed, each argued rather than skipped, so round 2 can judge the reasoning. (1) O-002 proposes giving `vf_semantic_file` the same fence suppression as the authorization scanner "so the two views cannot disagree". Refused: the manifest view should hash MORE, not less. Suppressing fenced Decisions content there would mean an edit hidden inside a code fence no longer invalidates a capture, which weakens freshness to make two parsers cosmetically agree. The authorization scanner ignoring fenced text and the manifest hashing it is the correct asymmetry — one asks "is this in force", the other asks "did anything change". (2) O-004 proposes rejecting `Status: resolved` unless `Prior` names a finding recorded in an earlier round of that gate. Implementing that literally would reject the round-1 report already canonized in notes/opponent.md, whose O-006 resolution refers to the 2026-09-09 provisional review — real, recorded review input that was never a counted round. So the enforced rule is `Prior` must name at least one syntactically valid finding ID and may not be `none`, which blocks the actual attack (a brand-new data-loss finding neutralized as resolved with `Prior: none`) without invalidating legitimate history. (3) O-007 offers two alternatives; taking the second. Changing `vf_report_block`'s accumulation risks altering already-sealed round digests — including round 1's own 1ac95178… — so instead `vf_reports` refuses non-blank content after a completed round's end marker, and completion re-verifies every previously sealed round digest before publishing. - 2026-09-11 — O-002 reopened a SECOND time and repaired class-wide as T030o12, before spending round 2. The preserved local probe (notes/verification/handoff/round2-self-attack/) reproduced 8 erroneous authorizations in 20 cases against the stop-point source: T030o7 added fence/blockquote suppression to `vf_round_authorized` but kept the one toggled boolean its sibling parsers already used, and a boolean cannot represent a fence. Markdown closes a fenced block only with a run of the SAME character at least as long as the opener and carrying no info string, so an inner ``` inside an outer ````, a `~~~` inside a ```, a four-space-indented run, and a run with an info string are all content — and every one of them flipped the boolean, exposing the quoted waiver as live on both gates and both authorization forms. The repair is one shared awk function `vf_fence` (constant `_VF_AWK_FENCE`) that retains the opener's character and width; all six togglers call it, so the six parsers cannot drift apart again. The probe now reports `cases=20 mismatches=0` with all twelve controls unchanged.

Validators that ask *is this in force* — round authorizations, canonical round blocks, reality-check matrix rows, plan definition/family rows, task stages and task blocks — suppress fenced, blockquoted and indented content. Validators that ask *what must be covered* — `vf_ac_catalog` and `vf_semantic_file`'s input manifest — deliberately do NOT, because dropping an obligation, or losing an edit hidden inside a fence from a capture's input identity, is the unsafe direction. This generalizes the 2026-09-10 refusal to give `vf_semantic_file` the authorization scanner's suppression.

- 2026-09-11 — HTML-comment suppression must be code-span aware, and checking that BEFORE implementing avoided wedging the spec. A naive whole-line scan for `<!--`/`-->` would enter comment state at notes/opponent.md:211 — round 2's own O-002 Class-wide fix line, which quotes three openers and one closer inside inline code spans — and suppress the remainder of the canonical report, so `vf_reports` would then fail every later reserve, complete, reconcile and probe with no scripted recovery. Across every parsed artifact (003's spec/plan/tasks/STATUS/report, shipped 001 and 002, the golden example and all templates) the only comment tokens outside inline code spans are the two anchored `gate-round-end` markers. The tracker therefore strips inline code spans before looking for comment tokens, exempts the anchored marker, and suppresses a line only when it starts inside a comment or its own content begins with an opener — while a mid-line opener still suppresses the following lines, which is the shape O-002 actually demonstrates.

- 2026-09-13 — T030o25's acceptance criterion was CORRECTED mid-implementation, before any capture, and the correction is the point of this batch. As first drafted it read "the `LC_*` family is a closed literal list recorded present/unset", taken from the first of the two options round 4 offered. Probing the rule before trusting it showed it does not close the class: a fixed list records `LC_CTYPE` present in a shell that exports it and unset in one that does not, so the digest still moves between two processes reading a byte-identical tree — and `LC_CTYPE`/`LC_ALL` are precisely the names macOS Terminal, OpenSSH SendEnv and CI images export by default. Measured: with the literal list in place, `LC_TELEPHONE` and `LC_FOO` became stable but `LC_CTYPE` and `LC_ALL` still produced different identities. The enforced rule is therefore that MEMBERSHIP is exactly `verification_environment:` with no fixed list beside it, justified name by name: `PATH` is already pinned by each declared tool's resolved path and content digest plus the harness records, `SHELL` is never read because library functions run under an explicit `bash`, `LANG` and the locale categories are not read either since the harness pins `LC_ALL=C` where collation matters, and `TMPDIR` decides where temp files go rather than what a check concludes. This is the exact failure round 3 and round 4 both diagnosed — an acceptance criterion that is itself the wrong rule, implemented faithfully — caught this time by testing the criterion instead of only implementing it.

- 2026-09-17 — O-004's PREFERRED RULE WAS MEASURED AND REJECTED BEFORE IT WAS IMPLEMENTED, and that measurement is the point of this batch. The round-7 report and the handoff both prefer "stop `resolved` suppressing the count at :1013, count every recorded finding at its recorded tier, reconcile declared totals and verdict against that". Extracted status and severity per finding per round from the real `notes/opponent.md` first: that rule computes `blocking=6` for round 1 against its declared 5, `6/3` for round 2 against `3/2`, and `7/3` for round 3 against `3/1` — so it rejects three rounds whose digests are sealed and cannot be edited without wedging every later reserve, complete and reconcile. Round 4 onward are unaffected. This is the fourth time a proposed rule would have rejected the record that proposed it, and the second time the check caught it before any code was written. The enforced rule is therefore the report's own stated minimum, derived from the predicate rather than from the field: `Status: resolved` asserts a repair no data available to `vf_reports` can corroborate, so it may not buy a CLEARING verdict; "rests on" is read off the same predicate — the suppressed finding is one this gate's own ROUND record already counted, which is history the report making the claim cannot rewrite. A declared prior-review manifest is not a round and was never counted, so a first round may still resolve a manifest-recorded finding and clear, which is the shape the real round-1 O-006 has. The rule is gate-neutral by derivation (`CLEARED` and `READY`), which also gives the reality-check side its first clearing-verdict protection.

- 2026-09-17 — O-002 closed on both halves, the second of which was open by DOCUMENTATION and not by code. The obligation is derived from the whole production the parser enforces — `- <UTC> — <kind>: gate=<g>; round=<n>; after=<n-1>; by=<b>; reason=<r>[; revision=<v>]` — in which every clause has exactly one refusal path and no refusal path may be silent, so a clause added later inherits it. The gate clause at :2303 was the only one taking a bare `next`, and it is also the one needing a DISTINCTION rather than a note added blindly: `gate=<g>` ranges over the gates that exist, so another KNOWN gate is that gate's record and stays correctly silent, while any other value is a malformed candidate and is named with its line. The gate set moved into one library constant (`VF_GATES` plus `vf_is_gate`) so the guard cannot drift from the gates the CLI accepts. Candidate detection also now covers a `<kind>:` written without its trailing space, but only behind the anchored prefix, so the Decisions entry of 2026-09-04 that names `` `waiver:` `` and `` `spec-revision:` `` in code spans is still not a candidate and the refusal list stays readable — checked against the real STATUS.md, whose round-8 denial lists exactly the same five candidates as before. The authoring half is closed where it was actually open: `spec-gate.sh waiver` is now in README's command block and in the round-cap paragraph the four invoker documents share, and the guard that keeps it there DERIVES the subcommand set from the dispatcher in `scripts/spec-gate.sh` instead of enumerating verbs — the enumeration is exactly how `waiver` shipped with a `--help` line and no reader.

- 2026-09-17 — O-010's proposed normalization contained TWO defects that only probing the rule before trusting it could find, and a third that only mutation found. (1) The round-7 report specifies stripping "backticks, asterisks and underscores". Stripping underscores DELETES THE NEEDLE: `LC_` is one of the names the guard searches for, and a claim naming only the `LC_*` family — a complete statement of the superseded rule — escaped a normalizer that stripped it. Measured, then underscores kept; backticks and asterisks still stripped. (2) A bare participle is not a membership claim. This repository writes "`LC_CTYPE` and `LC_ALL` included" meaning "among them", and the first normalizing matcher read `tasks.md`'s own replacement text as the superseded claim, so the reverse direction now requires a passive verb phrase. (3) The negation window began AT the predicate, so "The snapshot no longer includes `PATH`…" — how English negates this proposition in the forward shape — read as the superseded claim; the window now reaches behind the predicate. Scope is taken from the enumeration the manifest itself uses (`git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard` filtered through `vf_bookkeeping_path`, minus `STATUS.md`) so it is the manifest set by construction. Correction to the round-7 report, recorded rather than quietly dropped: its `Where` list names `tests/test-gate-contracts.sh:56-62`, which is the GUARD and not a surviving claim — the live tree carried no survivor at round 7 and carries none now.

- 2026-09-18 — The floor's failure mode on this host is MEMORY, not disk, and instrumenting the second attempt REFUTED the obvious explanation rather than confirming it. Attempt 1 ran 2h37m and died inside `spec-evidence.sh` with 18.7 GB free. Attempt 2 carried a 60-second disk/swap sampler and died after only 33 MINUTES with MORE disk than attempt 1 (never below 15.1 GB against a 19.9 GB peak) and FALLING swap pressure (used fell from 3428M to 2571M across its run). A floor that dies sooner under better conditions is not being killed by its own footprint, which is what the first kill's timing had suggested. What both runs share is that each was started as a harness-managed BACKGROUND TASK on a 16 GB machine, and each was terminated with SIGTERM by the host watchdog. Consequence for the next session, and the single most important thing in this entry: do NOT start the floor as a background task from inside the agent harness — run it in a foreground terminal the watchdog does not supervise, for example by typing `! caffeinate -i -m -s bash scripts/spec-gate.sh floor <spec-dir>` so it executes in the user's own shell.

- 2026-09-23 — Three independent read-only review passes of the two repairs (`security-reviewer`, each in its own private copy; brief and report `notes/verification/reports/t030o39-o40-review-brief.md` and `t030o39-o40-review.md`) found a blocking regression in my T030o39 each time, all failing closed, plus minors. Every one was verified here before acting. Pass 1: the anchor came from `pwd -P`, which under bash 3.2 keeps the case a caller typed while git reports the on-disk case, so a spec path typed in another case made the spec's own captures inputs and a capture went stale during its own execution. My first repair took the anchor from git discovery instead, and pass 2 showed that this obeys git's environment: with `GIT_DIR` exported, as git does for hooks in a linked worktree, `rev-parse --show-prefix` prints an empty prefix (confirmed on this host), and folding read from `core.ignorecase` can be wrong in either direction, including the escape direction on a case-sensitive volume. Rather than patch git's environment, the class was removed. The anchor and `vf_physical_rel` are now string work on physical paths (`cd -P && pwd -P`, never git), and folding is asked of the filesystem itself: a case-swapped spelling of the spec directory names the same inode. Pass 3 then found that this probe saw no letter to swap in a letterless spec name such as `003-2027`, which `new-spec.sh` can produce, and that a symlink spelled as the case swap would pass as folding on a case-sensitive filesystem. Both are fixed by the reviewer-verified change adopted verbatim: climb to the nearest component with a letter, and treat a symlinked swap as an alias. The pass-1 minors were all repaired rather than deferred: the refusal and member skip fold case where the filesystem folds; `..` resolves physically; a declared repository root is refused by name; the contract sentence now states exactly what the code does; and fixtures cover non-canonical declared-directory spellings and a declared symlink loop. T030o39's acceptance text was corrected before any capture to state the final location rule.

- 2026-09-24 — T030o41's rule is derived from the identity, not from the three readers. A reuse-checked command's verdict may come from reuse only if it is a function of the input identity, so every read such a command makes of the repository must read what `vf_snapshot` hashes: the path set from ONE enumeration function, `vf_source_paths` (Git worktree root required, `git ls-files -z --cached --others --exclude-standard`, NUL-framed, a git failure is a failure), minus `vf_bookkeeping_path`, with content through `vf_semantic_file` under the path's repository-relative name. `vf_snapshot`'s path pass calls the same function, so the two enumerations cannot drift and the identity's bytes do not change. Applied: the O-010 scan enumerates through `vf_source_paths`, scans `vf_semantic_file` output and returns nonzero when it cannot enumerate, and every caller asserts its exit status. The AC-023 loop gives the analyzer each shipped artifact through `vf_semantic_file`, which is the shipped artifact as the identity certifies it; the dropped lines are REQ-009 bookkeeping, so "analyzer exit 0" is claimed for that view, measured here at exit 0 with the legacy-compatibility line for both 001 and 002. Whole-kit fixtures copy exactly the `vf_source_paths` set through a `copy_kit` test helper, so machine-local ignored files and `.git` never reach them. `templates/verification-contract.md` states the same obligation for project reuse opt-ins, which the kit cannot enforce for arbitrary commands. The layout clause gets its fixture: an in-repository spec at `specs/<name>` has an empty anchor, so its own `notes/opponent.md` moves the identity.

- 2026-09-24 — INCIDENT, recorded in full because the invoker caused it: a development run of the T030o41 suites in the live worktree DELETED the live `.specify/stack.yml`. Timeline: `tests/test-verification.sh` started at 09:40:49, `.specify/` changed at 09:42:33 with `stack.yml` gone (`git status`: `MD`), the suite ended at 09:42:37 still reporting 3/3. An independent reviewer noticed it; the run was stopped at once. It was reproduced in a standalone copy, bisected to this session's `kit_copy_cases`, and traced with `set -x` to `rm -f '' .specify/stack.yml`, the EXIT trap of this session's `copy_kit`. Mechanism, confirmed with a minimal script under `/bin/bash` 3.2.57: a subshell function run as the WHOLE of a command substitution, as `run_rc` runs it, that leaves by `exit` fires its EXIT trap after its locals are unwound, so the trap read the CALLER's `present` — the fixture's last loop value, a relative path — in the repository root. `return` and falling off the end do not leak, and no kernel function shares the defect: the only `exit`s after an EXIT trap there are inside awk programs. Earlier dev runs took the same path harmlessly because the loop's last value named a file that does not exist; no tracked path is missing. Recovery was byte-exact: the working-tree bytes came from the pre-repair reproduction copy, hash `4dc6c67e…`, equal to the `path` record the 08:4x live manifest holds for `.specify/stack.yml` (the index version, `5851cd0b…`, differs, so `git checkout` would have lost the unstaged edits), restored at 09:47 with status `MM` again. Fix: `copy_kit` and the O-010 scan no longer use EXIT traps; each owns one private directory removed on every path, and failure paths return. A regression fixture runs a failing `copy_kit` exactly that way with caller variables naming a sentinel, and the trap-and-`exit` mutant removes the sentinel and fails it. Standing rule from this: exploratory suite runs go in a standalone copy, and a suite run is checked for leaving its repository unchanged. Full record: `notes/verification/reports/t030o41-review.md`.

Three premises were measured first in the scratchpad. Under `/bin/bash` 3.2, an `exit` inside a plain `( … )` within a subshell function leaves that function's EXIT trap unrun, and a variable of the function stays visible after it. A temporary assignment before a function call reaches the function's child processes and is gone afterwards. Git 2.50.1 reads no system configuration at all under `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` included, while an exported `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, or without that flag a `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM`, naming a `core.excludesFile` replaces the default XDG excludes. Decisions: (R3-1) git's repository-local names are cleared for git's own processes, never for the snapshot's shell. `vf_snapshot`'s root check runs through `vf_git`; the index listing and every per-entry `cat-file` run in one inner subshell that clears the names once; `vf_source_paths` clears its own. The environment records then read the ambient values, so a project that declares one of these names under `verification_environment` because its check reads it has that value hashed like any declared name, as "membership is exactly the declaration" requires, and an undeclared one still decides nothing. Declaring one is not refused: a check can genuinely read one, and declaration is the documented route. Fixtures: a declared `GIT_PREFIX` with two values gives two identities and a `present` record; an exported `GIT_DIR` at a missing directory leaves the snapshot's identity equal to the unexported one, a parity that had a fixture only for `vf_source_paths`. Cost: three `--local-env-vars` lookups per snapshot. (R3-3) A fixture that plants git configuration pins all of it through one helper, `git_config_pinned`: `HOME` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` as planted, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` and `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` unset. Git's command-line configuration variables (`GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`, `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`) are repository-local names, already cleared when a suite starts. The XDG fixture exports a `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` and a `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` naming a `core.excludesFile` around both pinned calls, so the pin itself is proven. Unsetting `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` is redundant under `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` on this git, as measured, and is kept so the pin rests on no single variable's precedence; its removal alone is an argued equivalent in the matrix. A search of `tests/` for git configuration (`GIT_CONFIG`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `excludesFile`, `templateDir`) finds that fixture alone; the template fixture's `GIT_TEMPLATE_DIR` outranks `init.templateDir` and reads no configuration. (R3-2) A host-kit fixture binds every load-time alias that names a live-kit directory in its scope, so it judges whatever spelling its site reads, the exact pre-delta one included. A new helper, `on_host_kit`, binds `KIT_DIR` and `SCRIPTS` (the aliases `tests/helpers.sh` defines) to the host for the call. It is used at every call whose host is a whole kit; the golden-example call also binds `EXAMPLE`, and the usage-limits calls also bind `FIXTURES`. The two partial-tree hosts (002's notes for the tally, and the leftover-spec tree) hold no `scripts/`, so they bind `KIT_DIR` alone: their sites read the live kit only through it, and K12 and K31 kill the pre-repair spellings. The copy sites keep deriving their source from `KIT_DIR` at call time. Matrix K43 and K44 use the exact pre-delta lines, `cp -R "$SCRIPTS" "$DISPATCH_KIT/scripts"` and `cp -R "$EXAMPLE" "$spec_dir"`. (R3-4) `gate_dispatch_grammar_cases` copies through `copy_kit "$KIT_DIR" "$kit" scripts`, with a host-kit fixture carrying an ignored script. The member set was re-derived by search (`t030o41-r34-search.sh`, run as `t030o41-r34-search-pre.{log,rc}`, exit 0). (1) The locale categories vary BY DEFAULT between shells, which is the variation the 2026-09-13 rule designed the identity to tolerate. `tr`, `sed`, `cut`, `wc` and `od` read `LC_CTYPE`; `sort` and `find` read `LC_COLLATE`, `LC_TIME` and more; `date` formats `%p` through strftime's `LC_TIME`; awk and grep take character classes from `LC_CTYPE`; bash's own `[[ =~ ]]` uses the C library's regex under the shell's locale; and git's messages follow `LC_MESSAGES`. These are PINNED. Every command in the kit's `reuse_checks` (`tests/run.sh` and `tests/shellcheck.sh`) exports `LC_ALL=C` right after its `unset CDPATH BASH_ENV ENV`, before any other program runs, and so does `tests/helpers.sh`, so a suite run on its own, as a task's Verify runs, is pinned too. `LC_ALL` overrides `LANG` and every category, and gettext ignores `LANGUAGE` in the C locale. `tests/shellcheck.sh` is a member the approved parenthetical did not name. It is the kit's other reuse-checked command, and it was measured: ShellCheck's output is byte-identical, empty and at exit 0 under nine locale settings (C, POSIX, C.UTF-8, en_GB, de_DE, ja_JP in UTF-8 and SJIS, en_US.ISO8859-1, and none set), pinned or not, so there the pin changes nothing and makes that hold by construction. (2) `TZ` is consulted by `date` and by the kit's local-time arithmetic, but it decided no verdict: with the locale pinned, the full suite passes 147/147 under `TZ=Pacific/Kiritimati`, `Etc/GMT+12` and `America/St_Johns`. It is not pinned. Pinning it would stop the dispatcher's local-time path from running in the zone it runs in, and no measurement asks for it; this is recorded as measured, not proven. (3) Option variables that a program reads only when someone sets them decide verdicts even with the locale pinned (`o016-option-vars.txt`). `UNAME_SYSNAME=Linux` makes macOS `uname` print Linux, and the usage-limits suite then fails 20/27, because the kit branches on `uname`. `GREP_OPTIONS=-v` inverts macOS grep; it broke the suite runner itself, and that run was stopped. The pages also name `TAR_READER_OPTIONS`/`TAR_WRITER_OPTIONS`, `POSIXLY_CORRECT`, `GNUSORT_*` and `COLUMNS`, and `shasum` is a Perl program, which reads `PERL5OPT`. - 2026-09-27 — T030o43's rule, derived from the harness rather than from the sites round 12 named, and measured before adoption in standalone copies (`notes/verification/reports/o017-measure.{sh,log,rc,time}`, and `o017-measure-m3.{sh,log,rc}` with `o017-measure-m3keep-C.diff`). FIRST HALF, the pin. Its member is the harness itself: the file set the input identity records as `harness` (`vf_snapshot`'s harness loop), namely `lib.sh`, `verification-lib.sh`, `spec-run.sh`, `spec-task.sh`, `spec-gate.sh`, `sdd-analyze.sh`, `spec-ac-coverage.sh` and `spec-evidence.sh`, and every process they start that is not the captured command. Every grammar they parse is ASCII, so the C locale reads every record a UTF-8 or single-byte locale reads, and, unlike them, decodes every byte. Measured (M1): with the candidate applied, each reproduction case gives its C result under `en_US.UTF-8`, `de_DE.UTF-8`, `C.UTF-8`, `ja_JP.SJIS` and the ambient locale, end to end through `round --complete`, the analyzer, `spec-ac-coverage.sh` and the waiver parser. Measured (M2): `tally` for 001, 002 and 003 is byte-identical to the unrepaired kit's, output and exit, under C and en_US.UTF-8. Measured (M3): `vf_snapshot`'s records equal the unrepaired kit's, under both locales, except the harness records and the edited files' own path and index records. The first M3 check excluded only the harness records and so reported a difference; its kept diff shows only the eight edited files and the untracked helper the script had written, and the corrected check (`o017-measure-m3`) states that exclusion and compares the rest. The pin is placed once: `verification-lib.sh` pins when it loads, and each of the six harness entry scripts loads it directly after `lib.sh`, before any program reads a repository or bookkeeping byte. `spec-run.sh`, `spec-task.sh` and `spec-evidence.sh` move that load up, and `spec-ac-coverage.sh` loads it on every path, not only for `--through-stage`. The measurement called the pin from each entry script and from the kernel; the adopted placement puts the same pin before the same first parser, and the dev run and fixtures re-prove M1 to M5 on the adopted tree. `lib.sh` is unchanged, so 002's dispatcher, which sources it but not the kernel, and the provider CLIs it starts keep the user's locale. The caller's `LC_ALL` (a value, empty, or unset) is kept in the exported `SDD_HARNESS_CALLER_LC_ALL` (`unset` or `set:<value>`), which a harness process started by another inherits (`spec-gate.sh accept` execs `spec-task.sh`; the floor runs `spec-task.sh list`); a process re-reads it only when it starts with no record or with `LC_ALL` other than `C`. The one program the harness runs that is not its own parsing, the captured command (`vf_capture`, and `spec-run.sh`'s legacy run), gets the caller's `LC_ALL` back exactly and never sees the record (M4: unset, empty and `en_US.UTF-8` each reach the command as they were). A declared `LC_ALL` is hashed by the caller's value, as the unrepaired kernel hashed it (M5: identical records for unset, `en_US.UTF-8` and `de_DE.UTF-8`, and two values give two records). A caller who exports the record by hand gets exactly what exporting that `LC_ALL` would give, so it opens nothing. Consequences stated rather than discovered: bash's own matching in the harness is now C as well, so `nocasematch` folds ASCII letters only (a non-ASCII case-variant spelling of a spec's own record is hashed, which fails closed), and the capture loop's glob order is C's, the order every recorded run used (C.UTF-8 collates as C). SECOND HALF, exit statuses. The rule is that no parse failure reads as a passing result. Every harness parser whose output decides a record, an identity or a verdict, and whose failure would read as an absence that passes, reads its exit status and fails closed with a diagnostic naming the parse. The members are derived from every text-program call in the eight files (`t030o43-parser-search.sh`, run as `t030o43-parser-search-pre.{log,rc}`, 151 candidate lines) plus the harness children the kernel reads, classified in `t030o43-parser-classes.md`: M1 the prior-review manifest (`:955`), M2 the Decisions-log authorization parse (`:2465`, which fails closed but then claims no candidate exists), M3 the frontmatter reads that pick the contract, worktree or branch (a failed read selected the legacy path, which ticks a v1 task with `--evidence` alone), M4 the ID inventories that decide coverage (`spec-ac-coverage.sh` exits 0 on an empty inventory), M5 the sorts whose output file the identity or acceptance reads, M6 `spec-task.sh list` in `vf_evidence_current` and the floor, M7 the counts over harness records, M8 the analyzer's clarification markers, M9 `spec-evidence.sh`'s legacy records and artifact names, and M10 the tally's metric lines. Sites whose failure already refuses, display-only sites and warning-only analyzer lines are listed there with the reason. Umbrella-only readers stay recorded extent. `lib.sh`'s own helpers keep their contract for their other callers, and the harness reads the frontmatter it decides with through one checked reader. Grep's decoding failure never shows in its exit status (it stops matching at the byte and exits 0), so for grep the pin is the repair and the status read covers every other failure. The fixtures: the manifest cases refuse exactly as under C with every `LC_*` removed under `en_US.UTF-8`, `de_DE.UTF-8`, `C.UTF-8` and `ja_JP.SJIS`, through `vf_reports` and end to end through `round --complete`; the analyzer's marker and AC inventory, and `spec-ac-coverage.sh`'s AC inventory, refuse the undecodable byte as under C; every harness entry script runs its programs under `LC_ALL=C`; the captured command sees the caller's `LC_ALL` unset, empty or set; a declared `LC_ALL` gives two identities for two caller values; and, through a helper that puts a failing copy of one program first on `PATH` for one argument, a fault injected into each member parser fails its operation closed with its exact diagnostic. On macOS each locale must be one an unpinned awk cannot decode the byte under, or the fixture fails by name. Removing the pin, an entry script's early load, the restore, the caller-value record or any status read fails a fixture. The contract paragraph and the kernel comment are corrected to say that the harness runs its own parsing under `LC_ALL=C`, gives the caller's `LC_ALL` back to the command it captures, and never lets a parse failure read as a pass. Files: `scripts/verification-lib.sh`, `scripts/spec-run.sh`, `scripts/spec-task.sh`, `scripts/spec-gate.sh`, `scripts/sdd-analyze.sh`, `scripts/spec-ac-coverage.sh`, `scripts/spec-evidence.sh`, `templates/verification-contract.md`, `tests/helpers.sh`, `tests/test-verification.sh`, `tests/test-gate-contracts.sh`, `tests/test-spec-gate.sh`, `tests/test-sdd-analyze.sh`, beyond the five-file guideline as T030o41 was, because the members are one class across the harness. Order: the repair; a dev run in a standalone copy (the changed fixtures, then the full suite plainly and under `LANG=en_US.UTF-8` with every `LC_*` removed); one independent review; the mutation matrix; acceptance. Decisions freeze from acceptance through round 13's completion and tally.

The rule's first half is restated: every grammar the kit parses is ASCII; under C each byte is one character, so no byte can end a parse; and a byte >= 0x80 is in no character class, so a record written with a non-ASCII space or letter where the grammar wants ASCII does not match it. The obligation is that such a line or token never reads as an absence that passes: a parser that decides with it refuses it by name, or, where it decides what must be covered, keeps more. Applied: (1) the prior-review manifest pass reads by the canonical finding's own field grammar: a line that looks like a Finding heading, or carries `**Class:**` or `**Severity:**`, must parse as one, a Class must match the Class grammar and a Severity the severity list, a finding with no readable Class is an error, and the pass exits 3, which the status read added in T030o43 turns into a refusal by name. Severity stays optional: shipped fixture manifests carry a Class alone, as round 1's O-006 record does, and the real manifest and every fixture give each finding a Class. (2) A required report section's body is a line carrying at least one byte outside ASCII whitespace and the UTF-8 encodings of the Unicode White_Space characters, so an invisible body is still empty and text in any script is still a body. (3) The identity's Decisions section starts at the first line that begins `## Decisions log`, whatever follows on it, so the identity keeps more, never less; each shipped STATUS has exactly one such line, and the snapshot of this tree is unchanged by it. (4) `spec-evidence.sh` routes the contract three ways, as `spec-run.sh`, `spec-task.sh` and the kernel do: absent is legacy, 1 is current, anything else is refused. (5) An artifact name is every byte that is not blank or control (`[^[:space:][:cntrl:]]`), so a non-ASCII name is taken whole. F3: `vf_return_parse` reads the size with its status and requires a number. F4: a fixture assertion for each of the five guards, each with a matrix row; the manifest sort and `vf_ids`' sort gain diagnostics, and `vf_ids` sorts an explicit `-` so the fault device can name that one call. F5: the kernel comment, the contract sentence and the task block say what the code does, and the comment names the doctor, whose provider readiness probes judge by exit status alone. - 2026-09-30 — The review-3 repairs, derived from the mechanisms and MEASURED before adoption (`notes/verification/reports/t030o43-measure-9.{sh,log,rc,start,end}`, exit 0, 343 PASS, the live status outside `notes/verification/` unchanged; run 1, rc 1 on two faults of its own, a check string and a history comparison written as byte identity where the rule intends nine values to change, kept as `-run1`), in four kits built from the live enumeration: CUR, PRE, REV and D (CUR with the candidate pairs `t030o43-edits/kernel-6.pairs`, `spec-task-3.pairs` and `sdd-analyze-3.pairs`, generated from the files' exact lines by `t030o43-edits/genpairs.pl` from the texts in `t030o43-edits/session9-new/`), judged under C and en_US.UTF-8 with every `LC_*` removed; every case of the session-8 measurement of record gives its result in D. THE RULE. (1) Values (R3-F4). The mapped line decides what a line is; every value a member passes on, into a catalog or a record, to another program, or into a comparison with a value that did not come through the step, is taken from the line as written by the anchor the mapped line was matched with, and trimmed at its ends by the same whitespace. The members were derived by listing every value that reaches a `rec`, a `print`, an `emit` or an outside comparison in each of the sixteen programs that take the step: `vf_config`'s stage map; `vf_bindings`' plan cells (split from the row as written, with a whitespace-aware trim), its inventory's stage, its task pass's stage and every field (Files, Verify kind, Tests, Verify, Reuse, Refs, Integration owner) and the maps it reads back (split from the line as written, because the step maps every input line); every identity and finding field value of the main pass of `vf_reports`, its Where continuation and its legacy severity; `spec-task.sh`'s task-list stage and subject; and the analyzer's two stage readers (its umbrella tag pass matches the stage by substring only, so there the change is an argued equivalent). Where a written value is processed further, the tests on it read the same whitespace (`vf_wstrim` as the passes' trim, `vf_wsclass` before a Files entry's `(new)` suffix, `vf_ws` before a Ref's `plan §` test). Not members, each for its reason: the manifest pass and the waiver scanner pass on only values whose grammar admits no whitespace (IDs, Classes, severities, timestamps, a `revision=` compared with a hex digest), which read the same from either line; `vf_ac_catalog`'s text is a canonical form by design, every run of whitespace one space, and `vf_matrix_parse` compares only its criterion cell with it (item 6); a name ends at any whitespace by the recorded R2-F5 rule, so `spec-evidence.sh`'s artifact names and the analyzer's Agent path are read from the mapped line, and a display that quotes an Agent line it cannot read quotes it as read; `spec-evidence.sh`'s stage and the analyzer's AC inventory are matched by substring or by ASCII pattern only; `vf_fm_value` and the STATUS view already read as written. Measured: a Tests locator and its registry row written with a U+00A0 inside the file name bind, `vf_locators_exist` finds the file and the catalog holds it as written; a manual observation name written with a U+00A0 equals the return's; T001 with a U+00A0 after each comma of its Refs, a Files entry ending in a U+00A0 and `(new)`, a Tests value after a U+00A0 and its stage heading written with a U+00A0 on each side of Backend binds, with its stage Backend. (2) The manifest pass's fields (R3-F1, R3-F2). A field is decided by its label: a line whose first word, after indentation, a bullet or ordered-list marker and emphasis, is Class or Severity in any case, and which is an emphasized label or is not continued by a letter, a digit or a hyphen. Inside a finding such a line must be written `- **Class:**` or `- **Severity:**`, appear once, and carry a value of its grammar, which is the text after that label and nothing further; anything else is refused by name. Measured: each of R3-F1's and R3-F2's spellings is refused by name in D under both locales, the ordered-list one included, and review 3's field-rule measurement over every recorded `notes/*.md` of 001, 002 and 003 and every real manifest, with the emphasized-label trigger added, makes no line a candidate that is not a canonical field line. The canonical parser's `value()` takes the text after the FIRST bold label of its line, from the line as written, so a second label stays in the value and fails its grammar: a canonical round's `- **Severity:** data-loss; **Prior:** minor` is refused as an unknown severity, where CUR counted it minor. Measured on the history: every tally, and every field of every recorded report catalog except the free-text Scenario and Reproduced values, are identical in D and CUR; nine such values in 003's opponent report (eight Scenario, one Reproduced), each quoting a bold label, are now the whole text where the greedy reading kept only the part after the last quoted label (each old value a suffix of the new), and no catalog reader reads those two fields (`vf_tally` reads the type, ID, Class, status, Prior, severity and Where). (3) Headings (R3-F3): a line not read as an ATX heading whose text, after leading whitespace or non-ASCII bytes, is a `#` run and the word Finding (any case, not continued by a letter) is refused by name; measured over six spellings after a Class-only finding (a U+00A0 after the hashes; a tab, a U+200B or a U+00A0 before them; no space after them; four spaces of indentation). (4) Outside any finding (R3-F5): the written spelling is refused as before, and any other Class or Severity line is prose until a finding ID appears after the last heading, on the heading or any line, and refused by name from then on. CORRECTION to review 3's proposed fix, which made every non-canonical line outside a finding prose: measured, that fix would drop in silence a finding that combines two R2-F2 spellings, a heading the pass does not open (a word before Finding, a bold Finding line with no heading, a colon after Finding) with unbolded fields, which CUR refuses; the ID condition refuses each of them by name, because only a finding's record can carry a Class or a tier. Measured: R3-F5's summary list and introduction parse; its nested sub-bullet inside a finding stays refused by name as a second Severity line, deliberately, because inside a finding such a line may be the tier. (5) Default field splitting (R3-F6): no member splits a mapped line by awk's default field splitting; the round heading's number, the totals line, the canonical Finding heading's ID and `vf_ac_catalog`'s continuation test read through `vf_fields` (ASCII blanks) or a pattern, and the canonical Finding ID is taken from the line as written, so its refusal names the bytes the author wrote. Measured: the D kit reads a Finding heading and a totals line carrying a U+00A0 identically on macOS awk and in the container's mawk. (6) The matrix criterion (R3-F8): the criterion cell is read by the AC catalog's own whitespace rule before it is compared; measured, a cell written with the U+00A0, an ASCII space or two spaces equals the catalog, and 003's sealed round-1 matrix cells are identical. (7) Stages (R3-F11): every reader of a stage heading that compares or decides with it takes its text as written, trimmed at both ends (`vf_heading_text`): `vf_config`, the inventory, the task pass, `spec-task.sh`'s list and the analyzer; measured end to end through `through-stage` for a trailing U+00A0 and a trailing ASCII space. (8) E02 (R3-F9) becomes a kill row, judged by a checkpoint of a spec whose contract is written with a U+00A0 before `1` and whose task lost a binding, which must be refused on the lost binding. History, D against CUR under both locales: `tally`, the analyzer and `spec-ac-coverage.sh` for 001, 002 and 003, `spec-evidence.sh` for 001 and 002, `spec-task.sh list`, the plan and task binding catalogs and `vf_config`'s stage maps for all three, the report catalogs as above, 003's sealed matrix cells, the round-13 and round-14 waiver checks and every STATUS semantic view are identical; the snapshot records outside the three edited files and the harness records are identical and D gives one digest in both locales; the manifest pass over all 18 recorded notes files and manifests, read as manifests, gives identical records and diagnostics. Recorded, not repaired: R3-F7 and R3-F10 (open extent, entry above); a finding opened by a line with no `#` run inside another finding (a bold Finding line) whose Severity follows a Class-only finding still falls into that finding, which is R3-F3's precondition beyond the heading catch-all and is recorded extent; a heading with no space after its `##` (not a CommonMark heading) is a stage to the inventory and not to `vf_config`, pre-existing. Fixtures: each guard gets an assertion with its exact diagnostic in the suite functions the task already binds (`report_manifest_locale_cases`, `analyzer_locale_and_fault_cases`, `checkpoint_parse_fault_cases`, `harness_locale_cases`), and matrix v4 a kill row for it; a default-split replacement that reads the same bytes on macOS awk (the totals line, the round number, the continuation test) is an argued equivalent, measured on mawk. The task's Files list is unchanged. Next, in order: the task block's Acceptance, corrected before any code (next entry); the pairs files applied, then fixtures and matrix v4; dev runs (quick, then full and full-locale in parallel); one short focused review 4; the matrix of record; freeze, capture, return, accept; one fresh floor on AC; round 13 under the recorded 2026-09-27T15:55:36Z authorization.

- 2026-09-30 — Session 10 of T030o43: the capture of record FAILED, the cause was reproduced, and the user chose the class-wide repair. The entry state (`notes/verification/reports/t030o43-entry-10.txt`) and the freeze point (`t030o43-freeze.txt`) equal `t030o43-handoff-state-9.txt`, the host on AC at entry. CAP-521-Q8OdIp (`t030o43-capture.{log,rc,start,end}`, input `ecbca9d2…`, the frozen source) exited 1; the task notification reported exit 0, which was the wrapper's. `tests/test-sdd-analyze.sh` ran 18/19, failing `test_verification_bindings` on six assertions of T030o43's own fault fixture `analyzer_locale_and_fault_cases`, while every other suite and ShellCheck passed. Nothing was accepted or ticked. The cause, reproduced in a standalone copy (`t030o43-tmpdir-repro.{sh,log,rc,note}`, with the two suite logs; its rc of 1 is the script's own miscount of five assertions for six, see the note): the fixture builds its fault matcher (`fault_bin … "$spec_dir/tasks.md"`) and its expected diagnostics from the caller's spelling of the sandbox path, which begins with TMPDIR. macOS's default TMPDIR ends in `/`, so the sandbox is `…/T//sddkit.*`, while `sdd-analyze.sh:31` canonicalizes its argument with `cd && pwd`, which collapses `//`, so the injected fault never fires and the printed path differs. With TMPDIR written without the slash the suite passes 19/19, which is how every dev run and both shards of the matrix of record set it, so none of them saw it. It falsifies the contract sentence (`templates/verification-contract.md:378`) and the kernel comment (near `scripts/verification-lib.sh:1917`) that TMPDIR decides "never what a check concludes": the kit's own reuse-checked suite concluded by its spelling. It fails closed here, and it is O-016's shape: TMPDIR is undeclared, so a PASS captured under one spelling would be reused by a floor sealed from a shell whose spelling fails a fresh run. User decisions (session 10): (1) repair the class within T030o43, not the one fixture: record the failure and a measured rule here first, then the repair, dev runs that include the ambient spelling, the matrix re-run, a new freeze, capture and acceptance, one floor on AC, and round 13 under the recorded 2026-09-27T15:55:36Z authorization, with no further focused review, since round 13 is the independent gate; (2) remove `$TMPDIR/cdk.out*`, another project's CDK synth output, before the floor (`r13-disk-reclaim-2.{sh,log,rc}`, exit 0: 546 directories, none held or changed within the hour, free disk 9.83 to 19.58 GiB, nothing else touched). Not authorized, as before: hosted CI without asking, shipped 002's reset parsing, the open-extent task, reality-check round 2, round 14, commits, the PR, merge, bypasses.

- 2026-09-30 — The TMPDIR rule, MEASURED before adoption (`notes/verification/reports/t030o43-measure-11.sh`; part a `t030o43-measure-11a.{log,rc,start,end}`, exit 0, 35 PASS; part full `t030o43-measure-11full.{log,rc,start,end}` exit 0, 9 PASS, its five suite logs kept as `t030o43-measure-11-<run>.log`; the O-010 scan pre-check `t030o43-o010-precheck.{sh,log,rc}`, exit 0: `superseded_environment_scan` reports nothing on CUR, and nothing on D with this batch's task-block correction applied; runs 1 and 2, rc 127 on a `timeout` macOS lacks and rc 1 on the case file written outside `tests/`, both faults of the pre-check, are kept as `-run1` and `-run2`), in kits built from the live enumeration: CUR (this tree) and D (CUR with `t030o43-edits/helpers-1.pairs`, `tv-5.pairs`, `vc-6.pairs` and `kernel-8.pairs`). THE RULE. Its member is every path a suite makes and every comparison of a path a program prints or receives with the suite's own spelling of it. Every such path begins with TMPDIR, and the programs under test canonicalize a path they are given, lexically (`cd && pwd`, which collapses `//` and resolves `.` and `..`: `sdd-analyze.sh`, `spec-ac-coverage.sh`, `spec-evidence.sh`, `spec-run.sh` and `spec-dispatch.sh` resolve their spec argument so) or physically (`cd -P`, `pwd -P`, as the kernel's anchor has since T030o39). So the rule is stated over the root, not over the comparisons, which are the enumeration this log records failing: when `tests/helpers.sh` loads, right after the locale pin, it sets and exports TMPDIR to the physical path of the directory it names (`cd -P && pwd -P`; `/tmp` when it is unset). The physical path is a fixed point of both canonicalizations, so every spelling of one directory gives a suite one string; it names the same directory, so where temporary files go is unchanged; and a TMPDIR that names no directory stops the suite by name at exit 2. Measured: (A) in D, eight spellings of one directory (as written, a trailing slash, a doubled slash, `/./`, `sub/..`, a symlink, a symlink with a trailing slash, a symlink with `sub/../`) and an unset TMPDIR each export the physical path, where CUR keeps every spelling and leaves an unset one unset; a TMPDIR naming no directory stops D at exit 2 with that diagnostic alone; (B) the member fails in CUR under a trailing slash on exactly its six assertions, among them the fault-injected task-reference read, and passes without one; in D it passes under a trailing slash, `//./`, a symlink with a trailing slash and no TMPDIR; (C) the new fixture passes in D under a trailing slash and unset, and is killed with its diagnostics when D gets CUR's `tests/helpers.sh` back, and, when the canonicalization is only lexical (`cd && pwd`), by the symlink spellings alone; (D) the input identity's records differ between CUR and D only in the four edited files' path and index records and the kernel's harness record; (E) ShellCheck passes on D; (F) the full suite and ShellCheck, each in its own copy left unchanged: in CUR, the census found no member beyond the one fixture, which fails on the same six assertions under a symlinked TMPDIR with a trailing slash, while an unset TMPDIR (`/tmp`, itself a symlink on macOS) passes every suite; in D, the full suite passes at 147/147 with ShellCheck under a trailing slash, under a symlinked TMPDIR with a trailing slash and with no TMPDIR. The contract sentence and the kernel comment are corrected (`vc-6`, `kernel-8`): TMPDIR decides where temporary files go, while its spelling (a trailing or doubled slash, a symlink) can decide a check that compares a path under it with the path a program prints, so the kit's own suites set it to the physical path of the directory it names when they start, and a project's check that does so pins it the same way or declares it. The fixture is in `reuse_command_input_cases` (`tests/test-verification.sh::test_capture_provenance`): the eight spellings, the unset case, the non-directory refusal, and a nested suite under a symlinked TMPDIR with a trailing slash whose sandbox must equal both canonicalizations of itself. Matrix v5 (`t030o43-mutations.sh`, v4 kept as `-v4.sh`) runs every judge under a TMPDIR ending in `/`, adds `tests/helpers.sh` to the byte-identical restore checks and `reuse_command_input_cases` to the baselines, and rows T01 to T06, each a kill: the canonicalization removed, judged by the fixture (T01) and by the member the capture failed on (T02); a lexical canonicalization (T03); the export dropped (T04); the refusal dropped (T05); the `/tmp` default dropped (T06). This session's dev runs use `t030o43-dev10.sh`, a copy of `t030o43-dev.sh` that gives every run a TMPDIR ending in `/`. Recorded, not in scope: a TMPDIR inside a Git worktree is a different directory, not a spelling (review 7's F1, already open extent); Linux, where TMPDIR is usually unset over a physical `/tmp`, was not run here. - 2026-10-01 — T030o44's rule, derived from what renders as a record rather than from the spellings round 13 named, and MEASURED before adoption (`notes/verification/reports/o018-measure.{sh,log,rc,time}`, exit 0, 504 PASS, the live status outside `notes/verification/` unchanged; run 1, rc 1 on two faults of its own comparison, is kept as `o018-measure-run1.*` with a note and the corrected comparison on its own files) on D, which is CUR with `t030o44-edits/kernel-1.pairs` (texts in `t030o44-edits/new/`). The mechanism is that both readers recognise a record line by an enumerated set of spellings and read every other line as prose, which is why four focused reviews of T030o43 and round 13 each found the next spelling. So the rule is stated over what a reader of the rendered Markdown sees and over the record the guards need, and a line a reader would read as a record that the pass cannot read is refused by name. (1) One label reader for both passes, `vf_label` in `_VF_AWK_LABEL`, part of the prelude of every parser of author text: a line carries a Class or Severity label when the first word of its rendered text is that label in any case and the line is an emphasized label or the word is not continued by a letter, digit or hyphen, which is the manifest pass's rule of T030o43 reviews 3 and 4 generalized. The rendered text drops inline raw HTML (comments, open and closing tags, processing instructions and declarations; CommonMark 6.6) and decodes numeric character references (2.5), and its first word is the one after any run of characters that are not letters or digits (indentation, heading markers, list markers, emphasis, backslash escapes), ordered-list numbers and task checkboxes. `vf_finding_heading` reads an ATX heading as a Finding heading when its text is the word Finding, in any case and not continued by a letter or digit, followed, after anything that is not a letter or digit, by a finding ID. (2) The prior-review manifest pass: every finding needs exactly one readable Class and one readable Severity (`finding <ID> has no readable Severity`), which closes at once every route by which a tier goes unread (a heading, inline HTML, a blockquote, a fence, a comment, or no tier at all) instead of naming them; a finding is the section its Finding heading opens, so a heading as shallow as that one closes it and a deeper heading is one of its lines, classified like any other (O-019: a field line under `#### Notes` is read, and a heading that carries a label, `#### Severity: data-loss`, is refused as a Severity line this grammar cannot read); and a heading `vf_finding_heading` reads that the pass cannot open is refused by name. (3) The canonical report pass: a heading `vf_finding_heading` reads, other than `### Finding <ID>`, is refused by name (`a Finding heading this grammar cannot read`) at any level and with any separator, and so is a line not read as a heading that is a `#` run and the word Finding, as the manifest pass already refuses it; a finding is closed by a heading as shallow as `###`, so a field line after a section heading is outside it; a field line outside any finding is refused (`a finding field outside any Finding`), which closes every opener that is not a heading (a bold line, a heading inside a list item) once its fields are written canonically; inside a finding, a line whose label `vf_label` reads as Class or Severity and which is not that field's line is refused (`a Severity line this grammar cannot read in finding <ID>`), so a second tier behind inline HTML, a character reference, a sub-heading or an unbolded label cannot pass the reader; and the duplicate-field key is the field's label, not the line's spelling, so an indented second Severity is a duplicate. Every new canonical rule applies to canonical rounds only and before the completion marker, so legacy rounds and the after-marker refusal are unchanged. Measured, D under C and en_US.UTF-8: every reproduction case is refused by name with its exact diagnostic (the heading and HTML spellings and the setext form as a Severity line this grammar cannot read; the quoted and absent tiers as a finding with no readable Severity; every canonical heading spelling, a U+00A0 before the hashes included, as a Finding heading this grammar cannot read; the bold line and the list-item heading as a finding field outside any Finding; `### Finding — G-042` as an incomplete finding, as on CUR; the indented duplicates as duplicate finding fields; the unread first spelling, a second tier behind `<b>`, a decimal or hexadecimal character reference and a sub-heading as a Severity or Class line this grammar cannot read in that finding); both O-019 shapes and a two-level sub-heading are read, so the CLEARED round is refused on the lowered tier and a CHALLENGED round at the manifest tier parses; the manifest siblings of the rendered text (a decimal and a hexadecimal reference, an inline comment, a second tier behind `<b>`, `### Finding — G-042`) are refused by name; every shipped plausible-manifest shape parses except `class-only`, which the rule turns into a refusal by design; the 2026-09-09 prior-review manifest parses with a catalog identical to CUR's; and canonical findings with a `#### Notes` or `#### Details` sub-heading, a titled heading, the recorded `**Class binding.**` lead-in, prose beginning `Severity was`, a quoted example and Held-up prose after the last finding all parse. History, D against CUR under both locales: `tally`, the analyzer and `spec-ac-coverage.sh` for 001 (tally exit 3, as recorded), 002 and 003 (kit path normalized), every opponent and reality-check report catalog with its diagnostics and exit, the round-13 and round-14 waiver checks, and the 368 snapshot records outside `verification-lib.sh` are identical. A survey of every canonical round first (56 findings in 003's 13 rounds; 001 and 002 hold legacy rounds only) found no Finding-like heading variant, no heading deeper than a finding's, no field line outside a finding or after a section, no duplicate or indented field key and no non-canonical Severity line inside a finding. The full suite on D fails one test only, `tests/test-gate-contracts.sh::test_reports_and_tally` (17/18), on fixture manifests whose findings carry a Class alone (the `class-only` row and the manifests the resolved-claim, Class-drift, `--complete` and `--reconcile` fixtures write at `:1417-1575`), whose refusal now comes before the diagnostic each asserts; every other suite and ShellCheck pass. Those fixtures gain a Severity line, which is the change the task block names. Consequences stated rather than discovered: a line inside a canonical finding that BEGINS with a code span holding a label (``  - `#### Severity: data-loss` is the heading form``) is refused, the safe direction, as the manifest pass already refuses it, so a report author puts a word before the code span. Recorded extent, not in scope: a finding written in prose only, with neither a Finding heading nor a canonical field line, is not a record to either reader, which is the recorded O-004 omission extent; a label written with a named character reference or split by a zero-width character is not read; Status, Prior and the other canonical fields are not label-checked, because only Class and Severity decide a count, a tier or a binding a later round can lower, an unread Status leaves the field missing and the finding incomplete, which already refuses, and a resolved claim already needs the gate's own history. Files: `scripts/verification-lib.sh`, `templates/verification-contract.md`, `tests/test-gate-contracts.sh`, `tests/test-spec-gate.sh`.

- 2026-10-02 — The repairs, derived from the mechanism and MEASURED before adoption (`notes/verification/reports/t030o44-measure-2.{sh,log,rc,time}`, exit 0, 767 PASS, the live status outside `notes/verification/` unchanged; runs 1 and 2 are kept as `-run1` and `-run2` with notes: run 1 failed on a fault in the candidate's own assembly and one in its comparison, and run 2 measured an intermediate candidate that the full suite on it then corrected in three places, each recorded in its note), on D, which is CUR with `t030o44-edits/kernel-3.pairs` (texts in `t030o44-edits/new-3/`), judged under `LANG=C` and `LANG=en_US.UTF-8` with every `LC_*` removed. The class is one mechanism: both readers decide by an approximation of what a reader of the rendered Markdown sees, and every blocking finding is a place where the approximation hides or drops a line a reader sees. Neither approximation can be made exact, because each is a line reader and CommonMark's containers decide what a line is (a fence opened inside a list item ends with the item; an inline `<!--` that does not close within its paragraph is literal text), so the rule makes an approximation error refuse rather than read. (1) The label reader reads every line twice and a label either reading yields counts (F1, F9, F11): as written, and as rendered, where inline raw HTML is taken out by CommonMark 0.31.2's own productions (an open tag with its attributes, a quoted value holding `>` included, a closing tag, a comment, which `<!-->` and `<!--->` complete, a processing instruction, a declaration, a CDATA section; one left open hides the rest of the line), a link's destination and title, a reference link's label and an image are taken out, a footnote reference renders as its number, a numeric character reference as its character and a named one as a character that is no letter or digit (no HTML named reference renders as an ASCII letter or digit, so no entity table is needed). An autolink or a non-tag is no tag by the production, so it stays, and its `<` is a non-letter, as it was before T030o44. (2) The deny side of each reader (F4): for the authorization scanner and the input identity's Decisions view, hiding a line denies, which is why the tracker suppresses what it cannot prove visible; for a reader of finding records hiding permits, because a hidden second tier is one the count never reads. So both readers refuse by name a line the tracker quotes that is a Finding heading, or that carries a record of the finding it is in (`vf_record`: a finding field line, a line whose label is Class or Severity): the manifest pass anywhere, the canonical pass in a canonical round of either gate before its completion marker. A quoted field line outside any finding attaches to none and stays quoted. The tracker itself is unchanged, so no sealed digest, authorization, Decisions view, stage map or binding catalog can move, and every divergence of the tracker from a renderer, the review's three and any other, refuses wherever it would hide a record. (3) The fields that decide a binding, a tier or a count hold their list item alone (F10): after a Class or Severity field line, and in a canonical finding a Status line, a following line that would continue its paragraph (anything but a blank line, a list item with text, an ordered item numbered 1, an ATX heading, a block quote, a code fence a renderer opens, a comment, an HTML block that may interrupt a paragraph, or a thematic break: `vf_block_start`), and any further text inside the item, is refused by name, unless the pass reads that line as a field line of its own. The check runs before quoting, because the tracker can quote a line a renderer continues the paragraph with. (4) F2 is recorded at its real extent: inside a canonical finding, a line whose first word, as written or as rendered, is Class or Severity and is not continued by a letter, digit or hyphen is refused unless it is that field's line, prose included (`class, not one spelling of it`; `- Severity: unchanged from round 12.`), so a report puts another word first; the reading of (1) can add such lines, never remove one. (7) F6: a legacy round that reaches every canonical rule parses with its catalog (its Blocks kept), and a comment holding `>` before a label is a row in both passes. F7: every refusal row of the manifest table asserts its own diagnostic (`t030o44-edits/fixtures-2.pl` with `refuse-diags.tsv`, checked against this kernel: each of the 56 refusal rows prints its own guard's message). F8: the contract, the kernel comments and the Acceptance say what the code does: a Class or Severity line (not every field line) is decided by its label; the rendered reading is stated as its productions; the first word follows any run of non-letters and non-digits; fixtures cover each reproduced member and sibling directly under both locales and each mechanism end to end. Measured: every review member and its siblings (an image, a reference link, a footnote reference, a destination with nested parentheses, a comment holding `>`, a fenced, blockquoted, commented or raw second tier, a whole finding hidden after `<!-->`, a quoted Finding heading in a Held-up section, a quoted Status line, a Status continued past its line, a backtick-info fence or a `<details>` block directly after a field, a nested note under a field) is refused by name with its exact diagnostic in D, under both locales, in the manifest and the canonical pass, where CUR sealed it; every positive parses, among them the shipped plausible manifests, the twelve returned round reports read as manifests, the 2026-09-09 manifest (catalog identical to CUR's), a quoted field line in a summary or Held-up section, a field line followed by a heading, a fenced block, a `<pre>` block, a thematic break, a blank line and prose, a nested field line, and a manifest field line after any of the 19 White_Space characters, which the pass reads as a field of its own (T030o43 review 2). Consequences stated rather than discovered: a report quotes an example of a Finding heading, or of a record line inside a finding, inline in a code span after another word, never in a fence, block quote or comment; a Class, Severity or Status item carries no continuation line, second paragraph or sub-bullet (one shipped plausible manifest nests a lead-in under its Severity line and becomes a refusal by name, the lead-in kept as a positive at the field level); five shipped fixtures that hide a whole finding (in a comment, a fence, a script block, or behind a mismatched raw-HTML close) now assert its refusal by name instead of its omission, which is stronger, since an exposed or read entry would fail them; a reality-check gap writes its detail as prose. - 2026-10-07 — T030o45's rule, derived from what decides which rules judge a line of a round rather than from the spelling round 14 named, and MEASURED before adoption (`notes/verification/reports/r14-measure.{sh,log,rc,time}`, exit 0, 1684 PASS, the live status outside `notes/verification/` unchanged) on D, which is CUR with `r14-edits/kernel-1.pairs` applied to the kernel and `r14-edits/vc-1.pairs` to the contract, judged under `LANG=C` and `LANG=en_US.UTF-8` with every `LC_*` removed. A round's style is set by its own text: the round heading makes it canonical, a report title read before its Round and Timestamp lines makes it legacy, and a Timestamp line makes it canonical, while every canonical rule of `finding()` and of both record readers reads the style of the line it judges and `flush()` reads the last one. The rule decides the style before the round has a body: once a round has a body (any non-blank line but its heading, its report title and its identity fields, quoted lines included), a title that would make a canonical round legacy (`a report title after the body of a canonical round`) and a Timestamp line that would make a legacy round canonical (`a Timestamp line after the body of a round its title made legacy`) are each refused by name, in a round of either gate. So in a round sealed canonical every body line was judged canonical, and a round judged legacy cannot be sealed canonical; a legacy round (002's titled rounds, 001's) never reads a Timestamp, so neither refusal reaches it. Measured: every reproduced O-020 case, the three siblings and the reality-check round are refused on D by name under both locales while each control keeps its own refusal; a titled round whose Timestamp precedes its body parses on both kits and is read canonical on D. Consequence stated rather than discovered: a round that carries a report title puts it, and every identity field, before its body; every recorded round already does.

- 2026-10-07 — T030o46's rule, derived from what a renderer shows as the first word of a line or of a paragraph rather than from the spellings round 14 named, and MEASURED in the same receipt. (1) Nesting (CommonMark 0.31.2 6.3, 6.4): the rendered reading reduces bracket pairs innermost first, to a fixpoint: an image becomes a character that is no letter or digit, a link or a reference link its text between two such characters, a footnote reference its number, any other pair its text, a task checkbox stays, and a bracket a backslash escapes is literal (2.4). (2) Emphasis inside a word (6.2): a run of `*` between two letters or digits is taken out; `_` cannot emphasize inside a word and stays. (3) Invisible characters: a second rendered reading also takes out every named reference, every numeric reference beyond ASCII and every byte outside ASCII, so a word a renderer shows whole across a soft hyphen or a zero-width character is read whole; this closes the extent sessions 12 and 13 recorded. A label any of the three readings yields counts, so each can only add a refusal. (4) Paragraphs (6.3, 6.6, 4.8): both readers of finding records read a label from each paragraph as well as from each line, through one shared step, `vf_para`, called on every line before quoting is read: a line joins the paragraph before it unless it is blank, starts a block (`vf_block_start`), follows an ATX heading or sits in a code fence, read without its block-quote markers; a line that continues a paragraph whose joined text carries a label its first line did not is refused by name, once per paragraph, as a Class or Severity line this grammar cannot read (in a canonical finding, and in a manifest as a second or unreadable field line) or, when the tracker quotes it, as quoted content carrying one; a label on the paragraph's first line was already judged as a line. (5) O-021 (4.5, 4.6): `vf_block_start` admits every HTML block that may interrupt a paragraph, every kind but the seventh, condition 6's block-level tag names included, as listed in 0.31.2 (`search` among them, `source` not), and a backtick fence of three or more whose info string holds no backtick; so a Class, Severity or Status line followed directly by such a block holds its item alone, while a line a condition-7 tag opens, and a backtick run whose info string holds a backtick, still continue the item and are refused. Measured on D under both locales: every reproduced member and sibling is refused by name in both passes (the manifest pass as `a second Severity line in finding G-042`, the canonical pass as `a Severity line this grammar cannot read in finding G-050`), and so are five invisible-character spellings (a raw zero-width space, a raw soft hyphen, `&shy;`, `&#173;`, `&ZeroWidthSpace;`) and two constructs spanning quoted lines (an inline comment, a block-quoted tag), as quoted content carrying a Severity line; the O-021 blocks (`<div>`, `<DIV class>`, `<details>`, `</details>`, a four-backtick fence) parse in both passes, and the `<span>` and backtick-info twins stay refused; every case table of `t030o44-measure-2.sh` gives its recorded result (its members refused with their diagnostics, except its `f10-details-after` row, which O-021 now parses; its positives parse; its two consequences stay refused); new positives parse on both kits (prose that wraps, a link and a linked image in prose, link text and a tag that wrap, emphasis inside a word of prose, a block-quoted remark, a wrapped Scenario). History, D against CUR under both locales: `tally`, the analyzer and `spec-ac-coverage.sh` for 001 (tally exit 3, as recorded), 002 and 003; every report catalog with its diagnostics and exit; every extracted round block, with the sealed digests of 003's fourteen opponent rounds and its reality-check round equal to D's blocks; the binding catalogs, the stage maps and configuration catalogs, the semantic views of every spec file, the round-14 (completed) and round-15 (exit 4) waiver checks, the snapshot records outside the two candidate files (366), the 2026-09-09 manifest's catalog, and the twelve returned round reports (rounds 2 to 6 and 8 to 14; rounds 1 and 7 have no returned report on file) and round 3's reconcile record read as manifests; the receipt's count check reads 14 under a label naming rounds 1 to 14, because its glob also matched the reconcile record, which it then read a second time by name, so 13 files were read, each parsing on both kits, and the label is wrong. The full suite on D, in its own copy with a `TMPDIR` ending in `/` (`r14-measure-suite.log`), passes every suite but one, 146 of 147 tests, and fails `tests/test-gate-contracts.sh::test_reports_and_tally` (17/18) on the shipped `value-details` row alone, under both locales: that row asserts the O-021 over-refusal and becomes a positive (next entry); ShellCheck was not part of the measurement. The tally of 003 takes 97 s on D against 98 s on CUR. Consequences stated rather than discovered: inside a finding, a paragraph whose rendered text begins with Class or Severity is refused, so a line that continues a paragraph is read with the line it continues; a fenced example is never joined, so a code example of a record line inside a finding is still refused line by line as before. Recorded extent, not in scope: the shared quoting tracker's divergences for its other consumers (unchanged); text a renderer hides by CSS or an attribute; a finding written in prose only (O-004); the readings stay approximations, now refusing wherever one of them sees a label.

- 2026-10-07 — T030o47's rule, derived from where CommonMark 0.31.2 places a paragraph boundary and starts a rendered line rather than from the spellings round 15 named, and MEASURED before adoption (`notes/verification/reports/r15-measure.{sh,log,rc,time}`) on D, which is CUR with `r15-edits/kernel-1.pairs` applied to the kernel and `r15-edits/vc-1.pairs` to the contract. A line reader cannot place every paragraph boundary where a renderer does, so the rule makes every boundary error fall on the refusing side. (1) Runs (4.3, 4.4, 4.8, 5.1, 5.2): `vf_para` keeps a run of non-blank lines that only a blank line, a line in a code fence or an ATX heading of at most three spaces of indentation ends (a heading inside a block quote included), reads each line without its indentation and block-quote markers, and takes every line of the run as a candidate paragraph start; `vf_para_label` names the Class or Severity line that the text joined from any earlier candidate whose own line carries no label makes of the run, once per run. A boundary the reader misses or invents cannot hide a label, since some candidate starts at the boundary a renderer keeps, and text joined past a boundary can only add a label. (2) Code spans (6.1): a third reading keeps the letters and digits of every code span (a backtick string closed by a backtick string of the same length, an escaped backtick opening none) and makes its other characters inert, so a bracket or a tag inside one opens or closes nothing, while its text reads as a renderer shows it. (3) Rendered line starts (4.6): the label is also read after each br tag and each condition-6 block-level tag, open or closing, outside code spans, in prose and, in the canonical pass, in the value of every field line of a canonical round. A label any reading yields counts. CORRECTION before adoption: the first candidate (`r15-edits/kernel-1-run1.pairs`) made every character of a code span inert, letters included, which would refuse a finding line whose first visible word is a code span's text followed by Class or Severity; its measurement was stopped in its last section and judges nothing (`r15-measure-run1.{log,time,note}`), and the adopted reading keeps letters and digits. Measured (`r15-measure.{sh,log,rc,time}`, exit 0, 975 PASS, the live status outside `notes/verification/` unchanged), D under `LANG=C` and `LANG=en_US.UTF-8` with every `LC_*` removed: every reported member, the ten siblings that seal on CUR and four members derived while writing the matrix (a code span after a br, an invisible character after a br or after a code span, and a closing p tag inside a line) are refused by name in both passes, and so is the Where-field spelling in both passes, while the controls and the two siblings CUR already refuses stay refused; new positives parse on both kits (prose that wraps, links, code spans holding brackets and tags, a code-span word before a label word, br and div tags in prose with no tier, a Where value with br and p tags and no tier, ordered and bullet lists, block quotes, an indented continuation line, a setext heading, an inline span, a blank line that ends a run, escaped backticks, a quoted heading before its attribute line, and the O-021 blocks), and a long run of field lines and nested bullets gives the same result and diagnostics on both kits; the 2026-09-09 manifest parses with an identical catalog, and every returned round report and round 3's reconcile record parse read as manifests. History, D against CUR under both locales: `tally`, the analyzer and `spec-ac-coverage.sh` for 001 (tally exit 3, as recorded), 002 and 003; every report catalog with its diagnostics and exit; every extracted round block, with the sealed digests of 003's fifteen opponent rounds and its reality-check round equal to D's blocks; the binding catalogs, the stage maps and configuration catalogs, the semantic views of every spec file, the round-15 (completed) and round-16 (exit 4) waiver checks, and the snapshot records outside the two candidate files (366). The full suite on D, in its own copy with a `TMPDIR` ending in `/` (`r15-measure-suite.log`), passes 147/147. The tally of 003 takes 102 s on D against 97 s on CUR. Consequences stated rather than discovered: inside a finding, a line after which the text joined from an earlier line of its run reads, as written or as rendered, as a Class or Severity line is refused, so a run of lines is read from each of its lines; a code span's syntax opens and closes nothing in that reading; and a line or a canonical field value whose text after a br or block-level tag begins with Class or Severity is refused. Recorded extent, not in scope: the shared quoting tracker's divergences for its other consumers (unchanged); text a renderer hides by CSS or an attribute; a finding written in prose only (O-004); a code span inside a word, which round 15 left to the user, as intraword emphasis was; the readings stay approximations, now refusing wherever one of them sees a label.

A run ends only at a blank line (2.1: spaces and tabs, a carriage return that ends the line read as its line ending) or an ATX heading, never at a line the shared tracker calls fenced, because the tracker opens fences a renderer does not (the derived member above); the tracker itself is unchanged.
